# Socket


## Public declaration inventory

Only names visible through the module’s exported scope are listed. Declarations from conditional branches retain their condition label; implementation-local declarations are omitted. Aggregate fields and enum members are recorded in the dedicated type-shape section.

- [OS == .WINDOWS] `FD_SET :: (fd: Socket, set: *fd_set)`
- [OS == .WINDOWS] `FD_CLR :: (fd: Socket, set: *fd_set)`

On Windows, `FD_ISSET` is an alias of Winsock’s `__WSAFDIsSet`; its callable signature is `(fd: SOCKET, unknown0: *fd_set) -> s32 #foreign ws2_32`. It reports whether the socket is present in the supplied descriptor set.

Variant 1 (Windows):

```jai
AI :: enum_flags s32 {
    PASSIVE                  :: 0x00000001;
    CANONNAME                :: 0x00000002;
    NUMERICHOST              :: 0x00000004;
    NUMERICSERV              :: 0x00000008;
    DNS_ONLY                 :: 0x00000010;
    FORCE_CLEAR_TEXT         :: 0x00000020;
    BYPASS_DNS_CACHE         :: 0x00000040;
    RETURN_TTL               :: 0x00000080;
    ALL                      :: 0x00000100;
    ADDRCONFIG               :: 0x00000400;
    V4MAPPED                 :: 0x00000800;
    NON_AUTHORITATIVE        :: 0x00004000;
    SECURE                   :: 0x00008000;
    RETURN_PREFERRED_NAMES   :: 0x00010000;
    FQDN                     :: 0x00020000;
    FILESERVER               :: 0x00040000;
    DISABLE_IDN_ENCODING     :: 0x00080000;
    SECURE_WITH_FALLBACK     :: 0x00100000;
    EXCLUSIVE_CUSTOM_SERVERS :: 0x00200000;
    RETURN_RESPONSE_FLAGS    :: 0x10000000;

    RESOLUTION_HANDLE        :: 0x40000000;
    EXTENDED                 :: 0x80000000;
    DNS_SERVER_TYPE_UDP      :: 0x1;
    DNS_SERVER_TYPE_DOH      :: 0x2;
    DNS_SERVER_TYPE_DOT      :: 0x3;
    DNS_SERVER_UDP_FALLBACK  :: 0x1;
    DNS_RESPONSE_SECURE      :: 0x1;
    DNS_RESPONSE_HOSTFILE    :: 0x2;
    EXTRA_DNSSEC_REQUIRED    :: 0x0000000000000001;

    AI_PASSIVE                  :: PASSIVE;
    AI_CANONNAME                :: CANONNAME;
    AI_NUMERICHOST              :: NUMERICHOST;
    AI_NUMERICSERV              :: NUMERICSERV;
    AI_DNS_ONLY                 :: DNS_ONLY;
```

```jai
    TCP_FIN_WAIT2   :: FIN_WAIT2;
    TCP_TIME_WAIT   :: TIME_WAIT;
    TCP_CLOSE       :: CLOSE;
    TCP_CLOSE_WAIT  :: CLOSE_WAIT;
    TCP_LAST_ACK    :: LAST_ACK;
    TCP_LISTEN      :: LISTEN;
    TCP_CLOSING     :: CLOSING;
}
```

### `addrinfo`

Variant 1 (Windows):

```jai
addrinfo :: struct {
    ai_flags:     AI;
    ai_family:    s32;
    ai_socktype:  SOCK;
    ai_protocol:  IPPROTO;
    ai_addrlen:   u64;
    ai_canonname: *u8;
    ai_addr:      *sockaddr;
    ai_next:      *addrinfo;
}
```
Variant 2 (Linux):

```jai
addrinfo :: struct {
    ai_flags:     AI;
    ai_family:    s32;
    ai_socktype:  SOCK;
    ai_protocol:  IPPROTO;
    ai_addrlen:   socklen_t;
    ai_addr:      *sockaddr;
    ai_canonname: *u8;
    ai_next:      *addrinfo;
}
```
Variant 3 (Android):

```jai
addrinfo :: struct {
    ai_flags:     AI;
    ai_family:    s32;
    ai_socktype:  SOCK;
    ai_protocol:  IPPROTO;
    ai_addrlen:   socklen_t;
    ai_canonname: *u8;
    ai_addr:      *sockaddr;
    ai_next:      *addrinfo;
}
```
Variant 4 (macOS):

```jai
addrinfo :: struct {
    ai_flags:     AI;
    ai_family:    s32;
    ai_socktype:  SOCK;
    ai_protocol:  IPPROTO;
    ai_addrlen:   socklen_t;
    ai_canonname: *u8;
    ai_addr:      *sockaddr;
    ai_next:      *addrinfo;
}
```

On Android and macOS, `AI` is `enum_flags u32`, `SOCK` and `IPPROTO` are `enum u32`, and `socklen_t` is `u32`. The public field order is the one shown above: canonical-name pointer precedes address pointer. Linux has the same field types but places `ai_addr` before `ai_canonname`; Windows uses `u64` for `ai_addrlen` and places the canonical-name pointer before the address pointer. These are the exported Jai declarations, including their enum types and ordering; they are not inferred from a host C header.

```jai
        #overlay (ifru_map) ifru_broadaddr: sockaddr;
        #overlay (ifru_map) ifru_netmask:   sockaddr;
        #overlay (ifru_map) ifru_hwaddr:    sockaddr;
        #overlay (ifru_map) ifru_flags:     s16;
        #overlay (ifru_map) ifru_ivalue:    s32;
        #overlay (ifru_map) ifru_mtu:       s32;
        #overlay (ifru_map) ifru_slave:     [16] u8;

        #overlay (ifru_map) ifru_data:      *u8;
    };
}
```

```jai

    ifr_name: [16] u8;
    ifr_ifru: struct {
        ifru_addr: sockaddr;

        #overlay (ifru_addr) ifru_dstaddr:         sockaddr;
        #overlay (ifru_addr) ifru_broadaddr:       sockaddr;
        #overlay (ifru_addr) ifru_flags:           s16;
        #overlay (ifru_addr) ifru_metric:          s32;
        #overlay (ifru_addr) ifru_mtu:             s32;
        #overlay (ifru_addr) ifru_phys:            s32;
        #overlay (ifru_addr) ifru_media:           s32;
        #overlay (ifru_addr) ifru_intval:          s32;
        #overlay (ifru_addr) ifru_data:            *u8;
        #overlay (ifru_addr) ifru_devmtu:          ifdevmtu;
        #overlay (ifru_addr) ifru_kpi:             ifkpi;
        #overlay (ifru_addr) ifru_wake_flags:      u32;
        #overlay (ifru_addr) ifru_route_refcnt:    u32;
        #overlay (ifru_addr) ifru_cap:             [2] s32;
        #overlay (ifru_addr) ifru_functional_type: u32;
    };
}
```

## Contracts and constraints

This module wraps socket initialization, blocking mode, keepalive settings, address formatting, and platform socket bindings. Windows uses Winsock handles and requires successful `socket_init`; Unix uses integer descriptors and `errno`. Nonblocking connect has a distinct expected error (`EINPROGRESS` on POSIX versus would-block on Windows). Methods return success booleans and log failures, but callers still own socket creation/close, address-family interpretation, and checking operation-specific errors. Keepalive options differ by OS; Switch 2 currently skips the detailed TCP tuning path.

### Operation-level behavior

| Operation group | Observable behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Initialization and errors | Windows `socket_init` calls `WSAStartup` for version 2.2 and must succeed before Winsock calls. Unix `socket_init` returns true without setup. `get_last_socket_error` reads WSA error state on Windows and `errno` on Unix; read it promptly after a failed operation. |
| `set_blocking` | Sets or clears nonblocking mode on the socket and returns false with a logged OS error if the native mode change fails. |
| `set_keepalive` | Disabling keepalive changes the socket-level option. Enabling it also applies idle/interval/attempt parameters where supported. Windows ignores the attempt count and passes the `idle_seconds_before_keepalive` and `keepalive_interval_seconds` values directly into Winsock fields whose units are milliseconds; the names therefore do not describe Windows units accurately. Switch 2 skips the detailed TCP tuning. A failure may occur after the socket keepalive flag has already changed, so false does not imply no partial configuration change. |
| `accept`, `bind`, `close_and_reset` | `accept` returns the native socket value and peer address; check for the platform invalid socket to detect failure. `bind` returns the native integer status (zero for success, socket error otherwise). Address family, port byte order, and address structure layout must match the target ABI. `close_and_reset` closes a valid descriptor/handle and resets it to the platform invalid value. |
| Nonblocking connect | POSIX commonly reports `EINPROGRESS` for a connection still being established; Windows uses `WSAEWOULDBLOCK`. These codes indicate pending progress, not a completed connection. |
| Address formatting | `to_string` emits numeric address text followed by `:` and the host-order port. IPv4 uses dotted decimal. IPv6 emits eight hexadecimal groups without zero compression. The result does not include brackets or a zone identifier. |

Most socket creation, connection, send/receive, option, and readiness APIs are direct OS bindings; their partial-transfer, interruption, and error semantics follow the target system call. Check returned byte counts and socket errors rather than assuming a full transfer.
